# Stage 6 — C2 / TOEFL Peak (Weeks 79–100)

Peak training on current TOEFL iBT (2026) sections. Secure ~5.5 then stretch toward 6. Full section sims and two mixed simulations. Week 100 taper. Optional Weeks 101–110 stretch noted briefly.

## Week 79 — Peak phase launch; scoring reality
**Level band:** C2 / TOEFL peak  
**Grammar focus:** 1–6 section scores; stretch to 6  
**Weekly pronunciation:** integrate sounds/stress/linking in warm-ups; Day 6 includes a short pronunciation recycle.  
**Study load:** ~12 hrs/week across Days 1–6 (Day 6 = review/integration heavier).

### Week 79 · Day 1 — Score scale literacy
**Level:** C2 / TOEFL peak  
**Time:** ~110–120 min  
**Goal:** Learn and use '1–6 section scores; stretch to 6' accurately while expanding vocabulary for 'Score scale literacy'.

**1. Warm-up (10–15 min)**  
- Re-read yesterday's checkpoint and mark yes/no honestly.
- Two-minute free speak on: 'Score scale literacy' (phone voice memo).
- Quick Anki: 10–15 due cards (new + review).
- Activate prior knowledge: list five words you already know related to today's topic.
- TOEFL micro: three to five warm items matching today's skill focus (unofficial practice acceptable).

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**  
- Grammar/point: 1–6 section scores; stretch to 6
- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).
- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (14 items listed): choose a response; conversation; announcement; academic talk; gist; attitude; function; connecting content; note-taking; campus; lecture; paraphrase; build a sentence; email
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (1–6 section scores; stretch to 6).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Score scale literacy'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Score scale literacy': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week79-Day1.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Did you complete today's TOEFL-aligned micro-set and write one process note (what slowed you down)? Yes/No.

---

### Week 79 · Day 6 — Reflect & commit
**Level:** C2 / TOEFL peak  
**Time:** ~110–120 min  
**Goal:** Integrate Week 79 skills on 'Reflect & commit' and verify retention with mixed practice.

**1. Warm-up (10–15 min)**  
- Re-read yesterday's checkpoint and mark yes/no honestly.
- Two-minute free speak on: 'Reflect & commit' (phone voice memo).
- Quick Anki: 10–15 due cards (new + review).
- Skim your week's error log; circle the top three recurring mistakes.
- TOEFL micro: three to five warm items matching today's skill focus (unofficial practice acceptable).

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**  
- Grammar/point: Spiral review — 1–6 section scores; stretch to 6; error correction; recycling Week 79 vocab
- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).
- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (12 items listed): choose a response; conversation; announcement; academic talk; gist; attitude; function; connecting content; note-taking; campus; lecture; paraphrase
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Complete a mixed worksheet: 12–15 items covering this week's grammar (coursebook parallel or self-made).
- Listening: one graded audio (A0–B1) or short talk (B2+); write five detail notes.
- Transform drill: rewrite eight sentences from affirmative to question to negative.
- Partner or mirror: five-minute Q&A using only this week's structures.
- Error clinic: correct eight deliberate mistakes taken from your error log.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Reflect & commit'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Reflect & commit': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week79-Day6.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- Weekly deck filter: review all cards tagged this week; suspend leeches and rewrite them.
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Can you explain this week's main grammar in your own words and use it in five original sentences without notes? Yes/No.

- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).

- Vocab (14 items listed): passage; paragraph; inference; vocabulary-in-context; main idea; detail; rhetorical purpose; complete the words; daily life text; academic text; skim; scan; choose a response; conversation
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.

  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (campus/workplace detail).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Note→answer mapping'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Note→answer mapping': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (passage, paragraph, inference, vocabulary-in-context, main idea, detail…). Tags: Week84-Day5.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.

**1. Warm-up (10–15 min)**  
- Re-read yesterday's checkpoint and mark yes/no honestly.
- Two-minute free speak on: 'Thesis in one line' (phone voice memo).
- Quick Anki: 10–15 due cards (new + review).
- Activate prior knowledge: list five words you already know related to today's topic.
- TOEFL micro: three to five warm items matching today's skill focus (unofficial practice acceptable).

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**  
- Grammar/point: positioning under word economy
- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).
- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (14 items listed): choose a response; conversation; announcement; academic talk; gist; attitude; function; connecting content; note-taking; campus; lecture; paraphrase; build a sentence; email
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (positioning under word economy).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Thesis in one line'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Thesis in one line': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week89-Day1.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Did you complete today's TOEFL-aligned micro-set and write one process note (what slowed you down)? Yes/No.

- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).

- Complete a mixed worksheet: 12–15 items covering this week's grammar (coursebook parallel or self-made).
- Listening: one graded audio (A0–B1) or short talk (B2+); write five detail notes.
- Transform drill: rewrite eight sentences from affirmative to question to negative.
- Partner or mirror: five-minute Q&A using only this week's structures.
- Error clinic: correct eight deliberate mistakes taken from your error log.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Week 91 review'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Week 91 review': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week91-Day6.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- Weekly deck filter: review all cards tagged this week; suspend leeches and rewrite them.
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Can you explain this week's main grammar in your own words and use it in five original sentences without notes? Yes/No.

- TOEFL micro: three to five warm items matching today's skill focus (unofficial practice acceptable).

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**  
- Grammar/point: ETS Practice Test style pacing
- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).
- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (ETS Practice Test style pacing).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Warm-up micro'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Warm-up micro': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week92-Day1.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).

- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (14 items listed): passage; paragraph; inference; vocabulary-in-context; main idea; detail; rhetorical purpose; complete the words; daily life text; academic text; skim; scan; choose a response; conversation
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (triad under official timing).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Targeted rewrite'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Targeted rewrite': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (passage, paragraph, inference, vocabulary-in-context, main idea, detail…). Tags: Week94-Day5.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Did you complete today's TOEFL-aligned micro-set and write one process note (what slowed you down)? Yes/No.

- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).

- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.

- Skim your week's error log; circle the top three recurring mistakes.

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**

- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).

- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.

- Complete a mixed worksheet: 12–15 items covering this week's grammar (coursebook parallel or self-made).

- Transform drill: rewrite eight sentences from affirmative to question to negative.
- Partner or mirror: five-minute Q&A using only this week's structures.
- Error clinic: correct eight deliberate mistakes taken from your error log.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Light speaking only: six Listen-and-Repeat strings plus two Interview answers; stop if voice fatigues. Voice-memo.
- Write: Light writing: three Build-a-Sentence items plus one short Email (skip full triad if fatigued). Focus on zero careless errors.

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week97-Day6.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- Weekly deck filter: review all cards tagged this week; suspend leeches and rewrite them.
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (14 items listed): listen and repeat; interview; intelligibility; pace; stress; linking; clear consonants; vowel length; natural rhythm; expand answer; personal example; organize; significant; approximately
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Controlled drill: ten gap-fills on (5.5 secure; stretch habits for 6).
- Matching: match eight vocab items to definitions or pictures; then cover and recall.
- Sentence transformation: rewrite six sentences changing tense, aspect, or voice as required by today's point.
- Dictation: write four sentences from slow audio (self-record, then play back) focusing on today's forms.
- Cloze paragraph (80–120 words) targeting today's grammar and six vocab items.
- Timed accuracy set: eight items in six minutes; mark and classify errors (form vs meaning).
- Paraphrase set: rewrite five sentences preserving meaning with denser or more academic wording.
- TOEFL-aligned micro-set: 6–10 items of the day's target task type; review explanations.
- Simulation segment under official pacing; no pause; log timing.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Peak Speaking: scored Listen and Repeat block plus Interview set aligned to 'Mindset & scoring honesty'. Voice-memo; compare to yesterday's clarity.
- Write: Peak Writing on theme 'Mindset & scoring honesty': timed Build-a-Sentence set plus Email plus Academic Discussion. Score with 2026-style rubrics (task, organization, language).

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (listen and repeat, interview, intelligibility, pace, stress, linking…). Tags: Week100-Day3.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Gate-related day: complete today's Produce tasks and save artifacts in your portfolio folder. Pass if tasks are complete and self-score is honest. Yes/No.

### Week 100 · Day 6 — Post-plan: weeks 101–110 stretch note
**Level:** C2 / TOEFL peak  
**Time:** ~110–120 min  
**Goal:** Integrate Week 100 skills on 'Post-plan: weeks 101–110 stretch note' and verify retention with mixed practice.

**1. Warm-up (10–15 min)**  
- Re-read yesterday's checkpoint and mark yes/no honestly.
- Two-minute free speak on: 'Post-plan: weeks 101–110 stretch note' (phone voice memo).
- Quick Anki: 10–15 due cards (new + review).
- Skim your week's error log; circle the top three recurring mistakes.
- TOEFL micro: three to five warm items matching today's skill focus (unofficial practice acceptable).

**2. Learn (25–35 min)**  
- Grammar/point: Spiral review — 5.5 secure; stretch habits for 6; error correction; recycling Week 100 vocab
- TOEFL alignment: practice must map to current iBT tasks (Reading: Complete the Words / Daily Life / Academic Passage; Listening: Choose a Response / Conversation / Announcement / Academic Talk; Writing: Build a Sentence / Email / Academic Discussion; Speaking: Listen and Repeat / Interview).
- Scoring honesty: aim stable about 5.5 across skills first; band 6 / historical '120' mindset is a stretch requiring near-perfect process.
- Vocab (12 items listed): choose a response; conversation; announcement; academic talk; gist; attitude; function; connecting content; note-taking; campus; lecture; paraphrase
- Examples (3–5 full sentences):
  - Prioritize question-type recognition before deep reading; map paragraph roles first.
  - For Choose a Response, identify the pragmatic function of the prompt immediately.
  - Email scores rise when every required content point is explicit and politely framed.
  - Secure section band 5.5 with stable timing; treat band 6 as a stretch requiring near-zero careless errors.
  - After each simulation, log only the top three leaks and retest them within 48 hours.

**3. Practice (20–25 min)**  
- Complete a mixed worksheet: 12–15 items covering this week's grammar (coursebook parallel or self-made).
- Listening: one graded audio (A0–B1) or short talk (B2+); write five detail notes.
- Transform drill: rewrite eight sentences from affirmative to question to negative.
- Partner or mirror: five-minute Q&A using only this week's structures.
- Error clinic: correct eight deliberate mistakes taken from your error log.

**4. Produce (20–25 min)**  
- Speak: Light speaking only: six Listen-and-Repeat strings plus two Interview answers; stop if voice fatigues. Voice-memo.
- Write: Light writing: three Build-a-Sentence items plus one short Email (skip full triad if fatigued). Focus on zero careless errors.

**5. Review (10–15 min)**  
- Anki: add today's new vocab (choose a response, conversation, announcement, academic talk, gist, attitude…). Tags: Week100-Day6.
- Anki: grammar pattern card — one model sentence plus cloze deletion of the target form.
- Re-speak the hardest sentence from Produce until smooth (maximum three attempts).
- Weekly deck filter: review all cards tagged this week; suspend leeches and rewrite them.
- AWL note: if any Academic Word List item appeared, mark productive (used in speak/write) vs receptive.
- Peak log: record timing, estimated section feel (1–6), and one process fix for tomorrow.

**Checkpoint:** Can you explain this week's main grammar in your own words and use it in five original sentences without notes? Yes/No.

## Optional stretch — Weeks 101–110 (overview only)

These weeks are **not** full daily cards. If you have completed Week 100 and still need polish:

| Week | Focus |
|-----:|-------|
| 101–102 | Reading leaks (Complete the Words, Academic Passage) |
| 103–104 | Listening endurance (Conversation, Academic Talk) |
| 105–106 | Writing triad precision |
| 107–108 | Speaking (Listen and Repeat + Interview) |
| 109 | Mixed light simulation |
| 110 | Final taper |

Keep the same daily rhythm if you wish, but recycle Week 79–100 templates with fresh official-style items. No new CEFR grammar syllabus.
